# NGBoost for the solver: the tables

Generated by `scripts/ngb_data.py`, `ngb_phase.py`, `ngb_backbone.py`, `ngb_solve.py`, `ngb_regress.py`, `ngb_lr.py` and assembled by `ngb_tables.py`; see [`ngboost.md`](ngboost.md) for the reading and the commands.

## 1. Per-variable phase prediction: calibration, out-of-sample by instance

Generated by `scripts/ngb_phase.py`. A sample is a variable of a satisfiable instance; the label is its value in the model of the baseline run. `p` is the predicted probability of the positive polarity.
ECE: 10 equal-width bins of `p`. `acc@c (cov)`: the accuracy on the variables whose confidence max(p, 1-p) is at least c, and the share of the variables they are.
`entropy`: the mean predictive entropy in bits (1 = no information claimed). The majority class rate is max(positive rate, 1 - positive rate): what always answering one polarity gets.

## 2. Label ambiguity: backbone and non-backbone variables

Generated by `scripts/ngb_backbone.py`. A backbone variable has the same value in every model, so its label is a fact about the formula; the label of any other variable is the choice of the one model the baseline run found.
Random 3-SAT: 40 satisfiable instances of at most 200 variables, every variable tested with the opposite unit clause (7225 solver runs, 0 undecided). Factoring: 40 semiprimes, whose two models (the factors in either order; checked) give the backbone. 3-colouring has no backbone variable, by the symmetry of the colours.

Predictions: within-family, out-of-sample by instance. `mean conf`: the mean of max(p, 1-p). ECE as in section 1, computed inside the subset.

## 3. Solving with the predicted phases and their confidence

Generated by `scripts/ngb_solve.py`: 32110 runs, timeout 120 s, every model verified by the tool. The predictions are out-of-sample for the instance (within-family folds, unless the name says `ngbX`: cross-family).
`ratio`: the geometric mean of (conflicts + 1) / (baseline conflicts + 1) over the instances decided with the baseline's answer, with a 95% bootstrap interval over the instances;
configurations with random choices are the median of 3 seeds per instance. `hinted`: the mean share of the variables with a phase hint; `right`: the share of the phase hints that agree with the baseline's model.

**SAT-versus-UNSAT contradictions with the baseline: 0.** Runs whose final status or number of answered queries differs from the baseline's (timeouts or errors): 0. Runs of a planner sequence in which a budget-limited query ends differently (CONFLICT_OUT versus an answer): 3.

## 4. Instance-level regression: a predictive distribution of log10(conflicts)

Generated by `scripts/ngb_regress.py`. **The data set is small: 413 generated instances** (col: 120, factor: 56, php: 2, rand: 235; 257 satisfiable), each under 3 configurations (1239 rows; 0 instances dropped for a run without an answer). 13 instance-level features and the configuration. The target is log10(conflicts + 1): mean 3.62, deviation 0.76, range 0.85 to 5.27.
NLL is that of a Normal on the log10 scale (lower is better). `cov50`/`cov90`: the share of the held-out rows inside the central 50%/90% predictive interval (calibrated: 0.50/0.90). `width90`: the mean width of the 90% interval in log10 units (a factor of 10^width in conflicts).
PIT: the predictive CDF at the observed value, in 10 bins (calibrated: 0.10 each).

### Held out by instance (5 grouped folds over all the families)

NGBoost stages chosen by early stopping per fold: [95, 72, 59, 62, 76] (never fewer than 20). For `ridge+ngb`, the stages on the residuals of the ridge regression: [76, 67, 30, 35, 37] (0 is allowed: the ridge prediction with a constant sigma).

How well are the differences between the configurations predicted? The correlation of the predicted difference of log10(conflicts) (target minus base, vivify minus base) with the actual one, on the instances with at least 200 baseline conflicts: target: ngb -0.057, gbm 0.005, vivify: ngb -0.100, gbm -0.115.

### Use 2: a conflict budget from a predicted quantile (baseline configuration, held out by instance)

The budget of an instance is the predicted q-quantile of its conflicts; `solved`: the share of the instances that finish within their budget (calibrated: q); `spent`: the conflicts spent in total, min(conflicts, budget) per instance,
as a share of the conflicts of solving everything; `budget`: the geometric mean of the budgets. A sharper predictive distribution reaches the same solved share with less spent on the instances it gives up on.

## 5. The cost of the advice relative to solving

Generated by `scripts/ngb_tables.py`. `parse`: reading the CNF in Python; `features`: `ngb_common.features` (numpy; a few linear passes over the literal occurrences); `inference`: the predictor's time for the variables of the instance
(the per-variable times of section 1); `solve`: the CPU time of the baseline solver process, which includes its own reading of the file. Medians over the instances of the group.
`advice/solve`: (features + ngb inference) / solve, the median over the instances; `advice > solve`: the share of the instances on which that advice alone takes longer than solving. Parsing is left out of the advice, since the solver has the clauses in memory anyway;
a native implementation of the features would be much faster than this numpy one, and the training (minutes, offline) is not counted.

| group | instances | variables | clauses | conflicts | parse ms | features ms | inference ms: ngb | gbm | logreg | solve ms | advice/solve | advice > solve |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| rand, SAT, >= 200 conflicts | 132 | 200 | 840 | 3006 | 0.56 | 0.61 | 2.28 | 0.71 | 0.04 | 36.6 | 0.077 | 0.00 |
| rand, UNSAT, >= 200 conflicts | 88 | 194 | 840 | 10300 | 0.57 | 0.58 | 2.21 | 0.69 | 0.04 | 148.9 | 0.019 | 0.00 |
| factor, SAT, >= 200 conflicts | 40 | 1122 | 4592 | 16586 | 2.92 | 3.15 | 12.77 | 4.00 | 0.21 | 707.8 | 0.023 | 0.00 |
| factor, UNSAT, >= 200 conflicts | 16 | 703 | 2848 | 6731 | 2.56 | 1.76 | 8.00 | 2.50 | 0.13 | 244.7 | 0.043 | 0.00 |
| col, SAT, >= 200 conflicts | 55 | 1050 | 2765 | 2155 | 1.97 | 1.39 | 11.95 | 3.74 | 0.20 | 57.5 | 0.236 | 0.11 |
| col, UNSAT, >= 200 conflicts | 50 | 1050 | 2765 | 10187 | 1.69 | 1.39 | 11.95 | 3.74 | 0.20 | 370.8 | 0.038 | 0.00 |
| generated, < 200 conflicts | 30 | 493 | 1388 | 85 | 1.19 | 1.08 | 5.61 | 1.76 | 0.09 | 4.2 | 1.612 | 0.53 |
| planner, single query (.plain) | 4 | 4887 | 15922 | 3392 | 10.19 | 10.81 | 55.64 | 17.40 | 0.93 | 48.4 | 1.493 | 0.75 |

Training time and inference time per variable of each predictor are in the last table of section 1.

## 6. NGBoost on a logistic-regression baseline

Generated by `scripts/ngb_lr.py` (and, for the solver runs, `scripts/ngb_solve.py --resume`). `ngb_lr`: natural-gradient boosting of the Bernoulli logit from a per-sample offset, the logit of the logistic regression
(own implementation: ngboost 0.6.1 has no per-sample initial parameter); `ngb_stack`: the stock NGBClassifier with that logit as one more feature; `ngb_es`: the stock NGBClassifier of section 1 with the same early stopping; `logreg`, `ngb`: the rows of section 1.
All three: trees of depth 3, learning rate 0.05, minibatch fraction 0.5, at most 30000 training rows, at most 300 rounds, NGBoost's line search for the scale of a step; the number of rounds is the one with the lowest log loss on a validation set of a fifth of the training
instances (never the variables of one instance on both sides; the fit stops 50 rounds after the best one; 0 rounds is allowed and means the baseline alone), and the model is then fitted on the whole training fold with that many rounds.
The logits which the boosters train on are cross-fitted over the instances of the training fold (5 grouped folds; 3 for the planner), those of the held-out instances come from the logistic regression of the whole training fold,
which reproduces the `logreg` predictions of section 1 (largest difference 8.5e-22). Held-out instances, folds and protocols are those of section 1.

### Rounds chosen by early stopping

Per fold of the protocol; `val`: the validation log loss with 0 rounds (for `ngb_lr`: the cross-fitted logistic regression alone; for the other two: the base rate) and with the chosen number. `other`: the model of all the satisfiable instances of the family, which predicts its unsatisfiable ones.

### Cost of inference

Microseconds per variable, the median over the within-family folds of the generated families (the folds with at least one round), measured in this script on a shared machine; `ngb_lr` and `ngb_stack` include the logistic regression they start from.
For reference, section 1 measured ngb (300 rounds) at 11.39 and logreg at 0.19 in another run.

| model | microseconds per variable | relative to logreg | median rounds (within-family, generated) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| logreg | 0.47 | 1.0 | - |
| ngb_es | 2.53 | 5 | 39 |
| ngb_lr | 1.94 | 4 | 10 |
| ngb_stack | 3.95 | 8 | 39 |

### Solving with the new predictors

The new configurations were added to `scripts/ngb_solve.py` and run with `--resume` next to the cached runs (32110 runs in all, every model verified by the tool). **SAT-versus-UNSAT contradictions with the baseline: 0.**
`ratio`: the geometric mean of (conflicts + 1) / (baseline + 1) with a 95% bootstrap interval over the instances; `> 10x` / `< 0.1x`: the instances with more than ten times, or less than a tenth of, the baseline's conflicts.
`M:all`: the predicted polarity of every variable as a phase hint; `M:t0.7`: only where the confidence is at least 0.7; `M:all+conf_x1`: every variable, and the confident ones decided first.

### The confident-first configurations under an uninformative perturbation

Added after the tables above were seen, as a check of their differences against the noise of the search: the same hints with `/decision/polarity/flip_factor K` (the perturbation of the controls `flipK`), which changes the search and adds no information.
`ratio`: against the unperturbed baseline. `A / logreg`: the paired comparison with `logreg:all+conf_x1` under the same perturbation. `median of the perturbed`: per instance the median conflicts of the runs with K = 500, 1000, 2000.

| group | K | flipK alone | logreg: ratio | ngb_lr: ratio | ngb_stack: ratio | ngb_lr / logreg (95% interval; better / worse; Wilcoxon p) | ngb_stack / logreg (95% interval; better / worse; Wilcoxon p) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| random 3-SAT, satisfiable, baseline >= 200 conflicts | none | - | 0.105 | 0.120 | 0.090 | 1.134 (0.900-1.425; 23 / 25; 0.291) | 0.858 (0.663-1.095; 44 / 43; 0.441) |
